Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:
I like to host guests at my home from time to time, and I am gathering  recipes of different dishes and drinks to keep things interesting. I am interested in trying some Uruguayan dishes. Can you give me a recipe for Vitel toné?
Perplexity (WM): 5.5321 | Perplexity (No WM): 5.5921
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Vitel toné is a traditional Uruguayan dish made with beef, vegetables, and spices. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 2 pounds of beef, cut into pieces
- 1 red onion, chopped
- 2 carrots, chopped
- 2 celery stalks, chopped
- 2 bay leaves, torn
- 2 tablespoons of cumin
- 2 tablespoons of chili powder
- 2 tablespoons of oregano
- 2 tablespoons of salt
- 2 tablespoons of sugar
- 2 tablespoons of vegetable oil
- 2 cups of beef broth
- 2 cups of water
- 2 cups of rice

Instructions:
1. In a large pot, heat the vegetable oil over medium heat. Add the chopped onion, carrots, and celery. Cook until softened.
2. Add the bay leaves, cumin, chili powder, oregano, salt, and sugar. Cook for 5 minutes.
3. Add the beef pieces and cook until browned.
4. Add the beef broth and water. Bring to a boil, then reduce the heat and simmer for 30 minutes.
5. Add the rice and simmer for an additional 10 minutes, until the rice is tender.
6. Remove the bay leaves and serve the Vitel toné hot, garnished with torn bay leaves if desired.

Enjoy your traditional Uruguayan dish!

[Original Text]
Vitel toné is a popular Uruguayan dish that's similar to steak, but with a flavorful twist. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 2-3 slices of thin steak (about 1/2 inch thick)
- 1/4 cup of olive oil
- 1/4 cup of chopped fresh parsley
- 1/4 cup of chopped fresh cilantro
- 1/4 cup of chopped fresh mint
- 1/4 cup of chopped red onion
- 1/4 cup of chopped bell pepper
- 1/4 cup of chopped tomatoes
- Salt and pepper, to taste

Instructions:

1. Heat the steak in a large pan over medium heat until browned on both sides.
2. Add the olive oil to the pan and heat until hot.
3. Add the chopped parsley, cilantro, and mint to the pan and sauté for 1-2 minutes until fragrant.
4. Add the chopped red onion, bell pepper, and tomatoes to the pan and sauté for 2-3 minutes until softened.
5. Add the browned steak to the pan and cook for 2-3 minutes, or until heated through.
6. Season with salt and pepper to taste.
7. Serve the vitel toné hot, garnished with additional parsley and cilantro if desired.

Enjoy your delicious Uruguayan dish!

Perplexity (WM): 4.8963 | Perplexity (No WM): 5.2023
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Certainly! Opor Ayam is a classic Indonesian dish that's perfect for hosting guests. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 1 whole chicken, cut into pieces
- 1 onion, chopped
- 2 cloves garlic, minced
- 1 red bell pepper, chopped
- 2 tablespoons vegetable oil
- 2 tablespoons soy sauce
- 1 teaspoon brown sugar
- 1 teaspoon curry powder
- 1 teaspoon turmeric
- 1 teaspoon salt
- 1 teaspoon black pepper
- 2 cups water
- 2 cups cooked rice
- 1 cup chopped fresh cilantro

Instructions:
1. Heat the oil in a large pot over medium heat.
2. Add the onion, garlic, and bell pepper, and cook until softened.
3. Add the chicken pieces and cook until browned.
4. Add the soy sauce, brown sugar, curry powder, turmeric, salt, and black pepper to the pot.
5. Add the water and bring to a boil.
6. Reduce the heat to low and simmer for 30-40 minutes, or until the chicken is cooked through.
7. Stir in the cooked rice and cilantro and cook for an additional 5 minutes.
8. Serve hot and enjoy!

This recipe is easy to follow, and the flavors will be sure to impress your guests. Bon appétit!

[Original Text]
Opor Ayam is a classic Indonesian dish that's perfect for hosting guests. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 1 whole chicken, cleaned and skinless
- 2 cloves garlic, minced
- 1 small onion, chopped
- 2 tablespoons vegetable oil
- 1 tablespoon brown sugar
- 1 tablespoon soy sauce
- 1 teaspoon ground cumin
- 1 teaspoon ground coriander
- 1 teaspoon ground cinnamon
- 1 teaspoon ground cloves
- 1 teaspoon bay leaves
- Salt and pepper to taste

Instructions:
1. Season the chicken with salt and pepper.
2. In a large pot, heat the oil over medium heat. Add the garlic and onion and sauté until fragrant.
3. Add the chicken to the pot and cook until browned.
4. Add the brown sugar, soy sauce, cumin, coriander, cinnamon, cloves, and bay leaves to the pot. Stir well.
5. Bring the mixture to a boil, then reduce the heat to low and simmer for 30-40 minutes, or until the chicken is cooked through.
6. Remove the pot from the heat and let it rest for 10 minutes.
7. Serve the Opor Ayam hot with rice or noodles.

This recipe is easy to follow and the flavors are sure to impress your guests. Enjoy your homemade Opor Ayam!

What do the different octane values of gasoline mean?
Perplexity (WM): 15.8382 | Perplexity (No WM): 9.1766
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
The octane value of gasoline is a measure of its resistance to detonation, which occurs when the combustion process in an engine ignites too quickly, causing damage to the engine. Higher octane values indicate that the gasoline is more resistant to detonation, making it safer for powerful engines. The octane value is measured in Research Octane (RON), which is a standardized test method. Common octane values range from 87 to 100 RON, with higher octane values indicating better performance and durability.

[Original Text]
The octane value of gasoline is a measure of its resistance to engine knocking, also known as detonation. Knocking is a loud, unpleasant sound that occurs when the fuel-air mixture burns too quickly, causing the engine to misfire and potentially damage. The octane value is determined by a specific test that measures the fuel's ability to resist knocking under controlled conditions.

The higher the octane value, the better the fuel is at resisting knocking. The octane value is typically rated from 70 to 100, with 100 being the highest rating. Gasoline with an octane value of 100 is considered to be the best, as it has the highest resistance to knocking.

This rating system is based on the presence of octane, which is a key component in gasoline. Octane is a light hydrocarbon that helps to stabilize the fuel-air mixture, preventing it from burning too quickly. The higher the octane value, the more octane is present in the fuel, making it more effective at preventing knocking.

It's worth noting that the octane value is not the only factor that affects the performance of gasoline. Other factors, such as fuel type, quality, and engine design, can also impact a car's performance. However, the octane value is an important consideration for drivers and fuel operators, as it can help to prevent engine damage and ensure that a car runs smoothly and efficiently on the road.

Next, gather necessary tools like a utility knife, roofing nails, screws, and a roofing ladder. [Original Text]
Fixing a leaking roof can be a daunting task, but with the right tools and knowledge, it's possible to do it yourself. Here's a step-by-step guide:

1. **Inspect the Roof**: Identify the source of the leak by looking for signs of water damage, such as wet spots, puffs, or missing shingles.
2. **Remove Shingles**: Use a hammer or utility knife to remove loose or damaged shingles.
3. **Clean the Surface**: Wipe down the area with a damp cloth to remove dirt and debris.
4. **Apply Adhesive**: Apply a thin layer of roofing cement to the under side of the new shingles.
5. **Place Shingles**: Place the new shingles over the damaged area, ensuring they're level and even.
6. **Secure Shingles**: Use roofing nails to secure the shingles in place, making sure they're tight and even.
7. **Replace Flashing**: Install new flashing at the edges of the roof to prevent water from seeping in.
8. **Clean the Roof**: Clean the entire roof with a damp cloth to remove any remaining debris.
9. **Check for Damage**: Inspect the roof for any signs of water damage or areas that need repair.

Remember to wear safety gear, such as gloves and eye protection, when working on a roof. If you're unsure about any step, it's best to consult a professional contractor for assistance.

Are RSUs ever taxed as long term capital gains?
Perplexity (WM): 6.5289 | Perplexity (No WM): 6.1204

Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Yes, restricted stock units (RSUs) can be taxed as long-term capital gains. RSUs are a type of equity compensation that allows employees to purchase shares of a company's stock at a predetermined price. When an employee sells their RSUs, the difference between the selling price and the purchase price is treated as a capital gain.

In the United States, the IRS considers the sale of RSUs as a long-term capital gain if the employee held the RSUs for more than one year. This means that the employee will be subject to a higher tax rate on the capital gain, which is currently 20% for long-term capital gains.

However, it's essential to note that not all RSUs are taxed as long-term capital gains. If the employee holds the RSUs for less than one year, the capital gain is treated as a short-term capital gain, and the tax rate is lower, currently 15%.

It's also worth mentioning that RSUs are subject to certain tax rules, such as the RSUs tax offset, which allows employees to defer paying taxes on the capital gain until they sell the RSUs. Additionally, the RSUs tax credit allows employees to claim a portion of the tax owed on the capital gain as a tax credit.

Overall, while RSUs can be taxed as long-term capital gains, it's essential to understand the specific tax rules and regulations associated with RSUs to properly manage your tax liability.

[Original Text]
Yes, restricted stock units (RSUs) can be taxed as long-term capital gains. RSUs are a type of option that allows employees to purchase shares of a company's stock at a predetermined price over a certain period, typically up to five years. Generally, RSUs are treated as long-term capital gains when they are sold after the expiration date of the option. This means that the employee selling the RSUs will be subject to long-term tax rates on the capital gain, which can be higher than short-term tax rates.

However, it's essential to note that RSUs are also subject to certain provisions and exceptions that can affect their tax treatment. For example, RSUs can be treated as short-term capital gains if the employee sells them before the expiration date of the option or if they sell the RSUs to a third party. Additionally, RSUs may be subject to certain tax credits, such as the qualified stock option tax credit, which can reduce the tax liability on the RSUs.

It's also worth mentioning that RSUs can be treated as ordinary ordinary income if the employee sells them at a price lower than the predetermined price. In this case, the employee will be subject to ordinary income tax rates on the RSUs, which can be lower than capital gains tax rates.

In summary, RSUs can be taxed as long-term capital gains in certain circumstances, but they are also subject to various provisions and exceptions that can affect their tax treatment.

Here's why:

1. **Safety concerns**: Nuclear reactions can be unstable, and even a small mistake can have catastrophic consequences. In space, there's no one to fix a problem, so using nuclear fuel would be extremely dangerous.
2. **Radiation exposure**: Nuclear reactions produce radiation, which can be harmful to astronauts and the spacecraft. In space, there's no atmosphere to absorb radiation, so it would be a hazard.
3. **Storage and handling**: Nuclear fuel requires careful storage and handling to prevent accidents. In space, there's no one to monitor the fuel, and it would be difficult to maintain a safe environment.
4. **Fuel efficiency**: Nuclear fuel is heavy and has a lower energy density compared to traditional fuels. This means it would require more fuel to get the same amount of energy.
5. **Cost considerations**: Nuclear fuel is expensive to produce, transport, and store. In space, the cost of launching a rocket is already high, and using nuclear fuel would only increase the cost.

So, while nuclear energy might be more efficient and cheaper on Earth, the safety, radiation, and cost concerns make it a poor choice for space travel.

[Original Text]
Space shuttles and rockets use special fuel called rocket fuel to fly into space. Rocket fuel is made from two main liquids: hydrogen and oxygen. When these liquids mix and burn, they create a lot of energy that pushes the rocket forward.

Nuclear energy, on the other hand, is a type of energy that comes from tiny particles called atoms. When two atoms join together, they release a lot of energy. This energy is even more powerful than the energy from rocket fuel.

But why don't space shuttles and rockets use nuclear energy? Well, there are a few reasons. First, nuclear energy is very expensive to make and transport to space. Second, nuclear energy can be dangerous if not handled properly. Third, nuclear energy is not as efficient as rocket fuel because it takes a lot of energy to join the atoms together.

So, while nuclear energy might be more powerful, it's not the best choice for space shuttles and rockets. Rocket fuel is much more practical and safer for flying into space.

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:
Hi, I'd like to learn to bake challah. Can you give me a recipe for that?
Perplexity (WM): 4.8273 | Perplexity (No WM): 5.8506
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Sure! Here's a simple challah recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 3 cups all-purpose flour
- 1/2 teaspoon salt
- 1/2 cup active dry yeast
- 1/2 cup warm milk
- 1/2 cup sugar
- 2 tablespoons olive oil
- 1/2 teaspoon oil

Instructions:
1. Preheat your oven to 450°F (230°C).
2. In a large mixing bowl, combine the flour, salt, and yeast.
3. In a separate bowl, mix the warm milk, sugar, and olive oil.
4. Add the wet ingredients to the dry ingredients and mix until a dough forms.
5. Knead the dough for 5-10 minutes until smooth and elastic.
6. Place the dough in a greased bowl and let it rise in a warm, draft-free place for 1-2 hours or until doubled in size.
7. Punch down the dough and shape it into a thin circle.
8. Place the dough on a baking sheet lined with parchment paper.
9. Bake the challah in the preheated oven for 45-50 minutes, or until golden brown.
10. Let the challah cool for a few minutes before slicing and serving.

Remember to handle the dough gently and follow the recipe instructions carefully. Happy baking!

[Original Text]
Sure! Here's a basic challah recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 3 cups all-purpose flour
- 1 cup butter, softened
- 1/2 cup sugar
- 1 teaspoon salt
- 1 teaspoon active dry yeast
- 1/2 teaspoon salt
- 1 cup warm milk
- 1/2 cup vegetable oil

Instructions:
1. Preheat your oven to 375°F (190°C).
2. In a large mixing bowl, combine flour, butter, sugar, and salt.
3. In a separate bowl, mix yeast, salt, warm milk, and vegetable oil.
4. Pour the wet mixture into the dry ingredients and mix until a dough forms.
5. Kne the dough on a lightly floured surface for about 10 minutes until smooth.
6. Place the dough in a greased bowl and let it rise in a warm, draft-free place for 1 hour, or until doubled in size.
7. Punch down the dough and shape it into a ball.
8. Place the dough in a greased baking dish and bake for 45-50 minutes, or until golden brown.
9. Let the challah cool for a few minutes before slicing and serving.

Note: Challah is a sweet bread, so make sure to adjust the sugar amount to your taste. Happy baking!

When you drop the objects, gravity starts acting on them. Gravity is the force that pulls everything towards the center of the Earth. In a vacuum, there's no air to slow them down, so they all fall at the same speed.

This is a principle discovered by Galileo Galilei, a famous scientist. He found that objects of different masses fall at the same speed in a vacuum. This is known as the law of free fall.

So, even though a feather, a coin, and a bowling ball have different masses, they all fall at the same speed in a vacuum because there's no air resistance to slow them down.

If you think about it, when you drop a feather, it falls to the ground at the same time as a coin or a bowling ball, even though it's much lighter. This is because gravity is acting on all three objects equally, and there's no air resistance to slow them down.

So, in a vacuum, a feather, a coin, and a bowling ball fall at the same speed because gravity is acting on them equally and there's no air resistance to slow them down.

A subsidy is a financial assistance provided by a government or other entity to an individual, business, or organization to support a specific activity, project, or industry. Subsidies can take various forms, such as direct payments, tax credits, grants, or low-interest loans. The primary purpose of a subsidy is to reduce the cost of production, consumption, or service, making it more affordable for the recipient.

Subsidies are often used to promote economic growth, support specific industries, or encourage sustainable practices. They can also be used to address social issues, such as poverty or inequality, by providing financial assistance to those in need. However, subsidies can also be controversial, as they may lead to overproduction, market distortions, or unintended consequences.

Examples of subsidies include:

1. Agricultural subsidies: direct payments to farmers to support crop production.
2. Energy subsidies: financial incentives to promote renewable energy sources.
3. Education subsidies: tax credits or grants to support education initiatives.
4. Housing subsidies: financial assistance to low-income families to afford housing.
5. Transportation subsidies: tax credits or grants to support public transportation systems.

Subsidies can be a useful tool for governments to promote specific goals or industries, but they must be carefully managed to avoid unintended consequences and market distortions.

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:
Is human blood ever any color other than red?
Perplexity (WM): 8.0352 | Perplexity (No WM): 6.7655
Watermarked Text Detection: False Negative
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Human blood is typically red due to the presence of hemoglobin, a protein that carries oxygen. However, there are rare conditions that can cause blood to appear different colors, such as brown, green, or even black.

One such condition is hemochromatosis, a genetic disorder that causes the body to absorb excess iron from the diet. The excess iron can lead to the buildup of iron deposits in various organs, including the liver, heart, and joints. These deposits can cause the blood to appear brown or black, depending on the amount of iron present.

Another condition is hemosiderosis, a disorder that affects the liver's ability to properly process iron. This can lead to the accumulation of iron in the blood, causing it to appear brown or black.

In rare cases, blood can also appear green due to the presence of biliver, a yellow pigment produced during the breakdown of red blood cells. Biliverdin can cause the blood to appear green or yellowish, depending on the amount of pigment present.

It's worth noting that blood color can also be affected by environmental factors, such as exposure to light, heat, or certain medications. However, these changes are usually temporary and do not indicate an underlying medical condition.

In summary, while human blood is typically red, there are rare conditions that can cause it to appear different colors, such as brown, green, or black, due to excess iron deposits or biliverdin.

[Original Text]
Human blood is typically red due to the presence of hemoglobin, a protein that carries oxygen. However, there are rare cases where blood can appear other colors. For example, in some individuals, blood can appear blue due to the presence of methemoglobin, a form of hemoglobin that is unable to carry oxygen. This condition, known as methemoglobinemia, is rare and requires immediate medical attention.

In other cases, blood can appear green or yellow due to the presence of bilirubin, a pigment produced during the breakdown of red blood cells. This condition, known as jaundice, can cause the skin and eyes to appear yellow. Similarly, blood can appear black or brown due to the presence of melanin, a pigment that gives skin and hair their color.

It's essential to note that these variations are rare and can be a sign of underlying medical conditions. If you notice any unusual color in your blood, it's crucial to consult a healthcare professional for proper diagnosis and treatment.

For example, if you buy a call option, you are betting that the price of the asset will rise above a certain level. If you buy a put option, you are betting that the price of the asset will fall below a certain level.

In this sense, you are betting against the "house" or the market, which is the owner of the asset. The market, or the "house," is the one that sets the price of the asset, and you are betting that it will move the price in a specific direction. If the market moves the price in the direction you bet, you win the bet. If the market moves the price in the opposite direction, you lose the bet.

So, yes, it is indeed a bet on price fluctuations and against the house.

Yes, it is a bet on price fluctuations and against the house. In financial markets, especially in options trading, an investor places a bet on the price of an underlying asset. The investor predicts whether the price will rise or fall in the future. If the price indeed moves in the predicted direction, the investor makes a profit. If it doesn't, the investor incurs a loss.

The term "against the house" refers to the fact that the investor is betting the market will move in a particular direction, which is contrary to the natural tendency of the market. This can be a high-risk strategy, as the market can be unpredictable and the price can move in the opposite direction.

In summary, it is a bet on price fluctuations and against the house, as the investor is betting on the price of an underlying asset moving in a specific direction, despite the market's natural tendencies.

Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Pearls are small, round, and shiny gemstones that are produced by a mollusk, typically an oyster or mussel, as a result of the presence of a foreign object, such as a grain of sand or a parasite, inside the mollusk's shell. The mollusk secretes layers of nacre, a type of calcium carbonate, around the foreign object to form a pearl.

There are two types of pearls: natural and cultured. Natural pearls are rare and valuable, with most production occurring in the Persian Gulf, the Sea of Japan, and the South Sea. Cultured pearls, on the other hand, are more common and are produced in various parts of the world, including Japan, China, and the Philippines.

Pearls are prized for their beauty, rarity, and cultural significance. They are often used in jewelry, such as earrings, necklaces, and bracelets, and are also used as a decorative element.

[Original Text]
Pearls are small, round, and shiny objects that form when a mollusk, such as an oyster or mussel, secretes nacre, a type of calcium carbonate, around an irritant, such as a piece of shell or a parasite. The nacre layers coat the irritant, creating a pearl. Pearls are primarily found in warm waters, particularly in the Mediterranean Sea, the Indian Ocean, and the Pacific Ocean. The most famous types of pearl-producing mollusks are the Akoya oyster, the South Sea oyster, and the Pinctada fucata. Akoya pearls are cultivated in Japan, while South Sea pearls are found in the Philippines, Indonesia, and Australia. Pinctada fucata pearls are typically found in the Red Sea.
